## OPEN

**⟶ FILED 2026-10-04 BY THE `U-ZONE-REPLACEMENT` / `PD-UI-14` LIVE RE-PIN PASS (its §7.2 limb 1 reading) — `PANE-HEADER-DRAG-NEVER-COMPLETES: NO PANE RELOCATES OR REORDERS ON THE ASSEMBLED APP`.** **OWNER: the pane-drag surface's own row, `PD-UI-4a`-class** (the decision row `DECIDED: ZONE-CONTAINER-ADOPTION-AND-THE-REPLACEMENT-BUILD` routes the pane-drag gesture surface to `PD-UI-4a`; this filing neither fixes it nor re-scopes it). **LAYER (`RCA-12`): APP — assembled renderer, REAL CDP input on isolated ports; NOT a node finding and NOT a package defect (no vendored byte is implicated).** **MEASURER: this pass, its own live run (`--block=uf_panes_12,uf_layout_10 --fixture=core`, scratch `--home`, isolated `--port`/`--cdp-port`, `--display=:0`) plus a read of `src/renderer/renderer.ts` / `src/shared/gesture-session.ts`.**
**SYMPTOM (MEASURED, and REFINED by the probe's second pass — the drag's own pointer audit).** A REAL CDP pointer drag (`Input.dispatchMouseEvent`) whose DOWN lands on a pane HEADER inside `zone:left` moves NO pane: the zone's pane-slot order reads `[doc-nav, search]` before and `[doc-nav, search]` after, at EVERY drop point tried (the following frame's top +26px · its midpoint · midpoint +8px · its bottom −4px · past its bottom +12px) and with BOTH path shapes (a single 40px jump; a gradual 2px→12px path that stays on the 28px-tall header for its first four moves). **THE GESTURE IS PROVEN, SO THIS IS NOT A PROBE GAP:** (i) the hit-tested start resolves back to that pane's own header through the APP'S OWN delegated resolution (`e.target.closest('.gutter[data-zone], .pane-collapse-toggle')` returns the header element the probe started on); (ii) a REAL click on that same surface DOES collapse the pane (`is-collapsed` appears), so the surface is live and hit-testable; (iii) **NO `gotpointercapture` event fires at any step of a 15-step walk (dy 2 → 240) and NO element holds a pointer capture at the end — even though SIX of the drag's moves land ON the 28px header** (`move targets=["pane-collapse-doc-nav" ×6, "pane-search", "advanced-search-toggle", …]`); (iv) **THE SESSION'S MOVE HOOK DOES RUN** — the zone's provisional drop-target mirror appears during the drag (`revealed zone mirrors=["zone:left"]`, i.e. the fork's `movePaneDrag` → the controller's reveal ran); (v) **THE TERMINAL NEVER ARRIVES ON THE ELEMENT:** the drag's `pointerup` lands on a NON-header element (`terminal targets=["pointerup:preempt-node-node-1466"]`) while the pane session's three tracking turns are bound to the HEADER, so no `end`/`cancel` turn is ever delivered and the drop never commits; (vi) **THE GESTURE'S STATE LEAKS:** the reveal set in (iv) is never cleared, so the zone stays `.is-revealed` — and a revealed empty zone suppresses the C11 empty-track collapse, which the SAME run's later `uf_layout_10` empty state measured (`zone:left cls="is-empty is-revealed"` with `gridTrackCollapsed=false`; the same row read `cls="is-empty"` / `gridTrackCollapsed=true` BEFORE the probe's gradual path reached the move hook).
**REPRODUCTION.** `node scripts/live-drive.mjs --block=uf_panes_12 --fixture=core --port=<free> --cdp-port=<free ≠ 9222> --display=:0 --home=<scratch>`; the block's own §7.2 limb-1 reading prints the drag start, the path, the slot orders and the pointer audit, and reads `NOT-DRIVEN` — `rearrangedInsideTheZone=false` with `gotpointercapture events=0` and `pointer-capture holders=[]`.
**SUSPECTED ROOT CAUSE (READ, NOT PATCHED — two named candidates in the same wiring; the discriminating measurement is (iii) above).** (1) **THE PANE SESSION'S TRACKING TURNS ARE BOUND ON THE ELEMENT, NOT THE DOCUMENT:** `installPaneSession()` builds its session over `shellEventSource()` (element-backed), while the GUTTER path deliberately builds `gutterGestureSource()` — whose own comment states the reason in the fork's words: *"a gesture whose pointer leaves its box must still receive its move and its terminal, or it is LOST (the failure this unit fixes)"* (`src/renderer/renderer.ts`, `gutterGestureSource`). That document-bound tracking was adopted for the gutter and NOT for the pane, and the pane's DEFERRED CAPTURE is claimed *from inside* `onPaneSessionMove` — i.e. only from a move that already REACHED the element — so a drag that leaves the header in its first few pixels receives no move, claims no capture and therefore receives no terminal. (The measurement above narrows it further: the move hook DOES run, yet the capture is never claimed — so the null-capture branch is reached with `captureEl == null` or the `setPointerCapture(pointerId)` call is refused/throws inside its own try/catch, and either way the gesture is left with no capture and an element-bound terminal.) (2) **A REFUSED ESTABLISHMENT IS SILENT ON THE PANE PATH:** the delegated `pointerdown` calls `session.begin(gestureEl)` and DISCARDS the answer (the gutter path checks it and reverts), and the session is a per-session singleton whose `begin` refuses `'busy'` while a stale slot is occupied — so one gesture that never reaches a terminal can latch every later pane drag in `busy` with nothing reported.
**PROPOSED FIX SHAPE (the pane path only; NO vendored byte may move — `R-3`).** (a) Give the pane session the gutter's own document-bound tracking (`gutterGestureSource`'s rule) or take the capture at establishment for the header surface; (b) honour `begin`'s answer on the pane path (revert/clear `paneDrag` and the latent `paneInstalled` record on a refusal, as the gutter path already does); (c) re-drive §7.2 limb 1's probe as the regression: a real hit-tested header drag must change the zone's pane-slot order and leave the sibling zones' and the stage's rects value-for-value unchanged.
**WHAT IT BLOCKS, STATED SO IT IS NOT READ AS A PASS.** `U-ZONE-REPLACEMENT`'s §7.2 limb 1 can only be reported `NOT-DRIVEN` while this stands (the limb is a real rearrange; the four sub-readings taken beside it are a SCOPED measurement of a state in which nothing was rearranged), so the unit's live re-pin cannot be green until the pane-drag wiring is repaired — by this unit or by the pane-drag row's own unit, at the architect's disposal.

NOT envelope) — OPEN (NEW)** | **A pane dragged from ONE ZONE to ANOTHER does not relocate; the same drag WITHIN a zone reorders correctly (proved live by the unit's `§7.2` limb 1: `before=[search,doc-nav] after=[doc-nav,search]`, sibling/stage rects unchanged).** So the gesture, the establishment, the capture-by-identity and the within-zone insertion all work — the CROSS-ZONE half does not. | **Live: the operator's own run (app on `127.0.0.1:3787`), plus the unit's `U-3` limb-1 reading as the positive control.** **SUSPECTED MECHANISM (to be confirmed by the diagnosis pass, not assumed):** the drop target resolution is zone-scoped — `insertionIndexForPoint`'s band rule derives an index within the DRAGGED pane's own zone (`slots` = the panes of that zone), and the cross-zone leg needs the relocate module's `candidatesFor`/`resolveTarget` seams (`createRelocateSession`), which this unit **DECLINED** (`§3.5`/`§3.6.1`, `docs/specs/unit-zone-replacement.md`) — so the target machinery for another zone's box was never composed. | **Compose the vendored `relocate` session's cross-zone half (the declined composition, now wanted), or extend the fork's own target resolution to the zones' boxes with the same scope-legality policy.** **OWNER: the follow-on interaction unit (the one `§2.1(e)`'s carve-out and `§3.6.1`'s decline named as inheriting the wired module).**  **⟨DIAGNOSED `2026-10-05` BY A LIVE PROBE PASS (`RCA-8(c)`, ANNOTATE-BESIDE — the suspected mechanism filed above is KEPT and is **FALSIFIED**): the missing `createRelocateSession` composition is NOT the cause.** **MEASURED ON THE ASSEMBLED APP (real CDP input, isolated boot; the operator's `:3787` instance untouched):** the gesture establishes, captures and tracks correctly (a real `doc-nav` header drag far right moves through the zones with `captures:[{'el':'pane-frame','id':1}]`), so this is not a gesture defect. **CAUSE (A): EVERY OTHER ZONE'S BOX IS DEGENERATE AND THE HIT-TEST REFUSES IT BY CONSTRUCTION** — `boxes: left {w:220,h:634} | right {w:0} | header {h:0} | footer {h:0}` with `tracks: left 220px | right 0px | header 0px | footer 0px`, because `applyZoneTracks` writes `--zone-<z>-track: 0px` INLINE for every empty zone and an inline declaration beats the stylesheet's own `is-revealed` carve-out, so a reveal can never give an empty zone an extent; `measuresWithinSnapThreshold` returns `false` when the box's span is zero and `dropZoneForPoint` therefore returns `null`, so the terminal takes `cancelPaneDrag()`. Measured: `dropZoneForPoint({939,370}, [the real four boxes], 24) = null` while the same point against a NON-degenerate `right` box reads `"right"` (and `distanceToZoneBox` reads `8`, inside the 24 threshold — the degenerate gate, not the distance, is what refuses). **CAUSE (B): THE REVEAL'S OWN RE-RENDER KILLS THE GESTURE** — with the target box held non-degenerate, the first reveal crossing fires `onRevealChange` → `rerenderAppGraph()` → `applyZoneTracks()` + `loadAppGraph`, which REPLACES the captured `.pane-frame`; the document `lostpointercapture` handler then cancels the pane session (`frame-removed pid=doc-nav`/`search`, then `lostpointercapture #document`, no `pointerup`, slots unchanged, zero `operatorSettings` writes). Control: the same drag that never crosses a zone keeps its capture and reaches its terminal. **FALSIFYING READING FOR THE FILED HYPOTHESIS:** the shipped bytes are cross-zone-correct — `movePane(layout,'doc-nav','right',0)` relocates and renumbers the source zone, `legalZonesForScope('app-graph')` = all four zones, `insertionIndexForPoint` derives the index from the TARGET zone's slots — and `createRelocateSession` is consumed **nowhere** under `src/**` while `withinProximity` is the module's only runtime import: the module takes the caller's MEASURED candidate list, so a geometry refusal would arrive there as an empty set and be reproduced. **MINIMAL FIX SHAPE (fork-side only, `src/renderer/**`, no vendored byte): (P1) a drop-target geometry source that exists for a COLLAPSED zone — the grid cell's own box, or a per-move track write for the zone under the pointer — keeping the unusable-box refusal as the LAST gate, not the first; (P2) a pane-drag lifetime that survives its own reveal's re-render — suspend `loadAppGraph` while a `paneDrag` is in flight, or re-resolve the capture/listeners after the frame is replaced (the wiring already re-resolves the frame by pane identity at the claim).** **LAYER: APP/assembled (`RCA-12`); a node reading is never this evidence.** |

persisted size 348` with **exactly ONE commit** and **zero writes during the moves** (the whole run's 10 DOM writes all land inside `applyZoneTracks` at the terminal). **CAUSE: the adopted composition a late install **reds loudly** (**flakiness, not a silent false green**). | **RECORDED READINGS; measurers: the two gate-4 adversarial passes.** **App side:** the finding is the app-side pass's `A-1` (`docs/specs/unit-app-harness-readiness.md` `§3a.2`, BLOCKING) — **the register cannot see it**, because `P-SM-1`'s `failed` arms **call `markBootSettled({ok:false})` directly** and therefore test the **state owner**, never the production sender. **Harness side:** the divergence pass's `A-1` (`docs/specs/unit-divergence-drive-fixture.md` `§3a.2`, BLOCKING) — **the same root cause**, escalating app-side because **no leg-side fix exists** (a re-probe would be a **NEW CHECK** — the harness's `F-1a` pin-drift rule — or a pin drift against the preserved comparison set, `C-3` item 1). **THE STRONGEST FALSE GREEN, both passes, quoted:** *an opt-in launch whose `boot.status` stays `pending` forever* — **drive: make `host.mountTab` throw after `boot()` resolves.** | **The `.then` body drops the promise it creates:** `bootTabs()` is invoked for its side effect and its returned promise is neither awaited nor caught, so the chain's rejection path is unreachable from inside it. **The observable's own state machine is not at fault** — the sender simply never reports — which is why a state-machine register row passes while the production path deadlocks. | **A FIX IS LANDING IN THIS SAME ROUND** (the app-side host fix; **owner: that landing pass**). **Shape:** the boot chain's settle call must be reachable on a `bootTabs()` throw — i.e. the `.then` body must route the throw to the chain's failure sender (await/`catch` the `bootTabs()` promise, or an equivalent that guarantees the `ok:false` signal), **WITHOUT adding a check to the divergence leg and WITHOUT changing the comparison set**. **Regression obligation:** a row that drives the production sender (not the state owner) — see the coverage row below. **The harness-side half is ESCALATE-ONLY** and is closed by this fix; **`§2.2 B-1` item 3, `§3.2 F-4` and `§0B.3` are NOT rewritten here** (the app-side over-read amendment is owed, filed below). |

the module never calls it, nothing is retained and no storage is touched"* (its citation: its `src/shared/focus-model.ts`'s `persist`, its `docs/guide/seams.md`'s `persist` row; OUR precedent is the vendored `src/shared/layout-projection.ts` + `src/renderer/layout-state.ts`'s `applyLayoutToRoot` path and the `W2.3` unit row that adopts it). **(2) A VERSIONED COERCE GATE ON READ** — *"version-from-first-write … a store payload carries its own schema version from its first write, so a later shape change is coercible instead of fatal"*; the write discipline to standardise on is the atomic shape *"`${path}.tmp` + `writeFileSync` + `renameSync`"* (the foundation's `module-store.ts` precedent, which our vendoring already carries as `src/shared/**` consumers) — **atomicity is the fork's own responsibility, since NO foundation helper implements or supplies it.** | **THE GAP IS OURS AND THE FOUNDATION HAS DECLINED TO SUPPLY IT (`../Provident-Electron/docs/decisions.md` `NO-FOUNDATION-CONFIG-FILE-FACILITY`: no generic userData store, no config-file API family, no `schemaVersion` token, no migration machinery — *"this repo ships no GENERIC persisted config — it does not ship 'no persisted config'"*).** **The class is a CALLER-side duty by ruling: the mechanism layer hands the value back and the CALLER writes it (`H-r16`, `S-d4`, `S-d8` prohibition 4 are the foundation's standing text, cited here, never amended).** **Our carriers were written at different times under different disciplines and no pass has ever swept them as ONE set** — so the inconsistency is a recorded drift, not a defect in any one store. | **OWNER: HOST (this repo). SHAPE — THREE CLAUSES, EACH TESTABLE ON OUR OWN TREE, AND NO UPSTREAM DEPENDENCY:** **(1) ATOMIC REPLACE on every carrier that writes a whole file** — the `${path}.tmp` + `renameSync` shape, with the non-atomic sites named and swept (the crash-truncation it prevents is the reason the shape exists). **(2) A SCHEMA/VERSION GATE ON READ for every carrier that has a shape which may change** — a version member from the first write plus a fail-soft coercion on read, the `coerceLayout`/`LAYOUT_VERSION` precedent generalised. **(3) THE PERSISTED SET GOVERNS THE RENDERED FRAMES** — the persisted state the app boots from is the authority for what renders (the `GAP-6`/`S-12` symptom class this repo records live: *"the persisted set does not govern the rendered frames"*); this clause is the reason the pass cannot be a file-writing chore alone. **AND THE WITNESS OBLIGATION THAT COMES WITH IT: OUR OWN LEGS MUST WITNESS OUR OWN FILES** — a third file is UNOBSERVED by the foundation's two-name witness, so any leg that reads our stores must be OUR instrument. **REVISIT CONDITION: the next host pass that touches persistence (`W2`'s layout-projection adoption, the `W5` zone/census writes or the `W6a` declaration writes — each SUPPLIES THE SINK AND OWNS THE WRITE, never a vendored module); the row stays OPEN until the three clauses are landed or a dated reason is recorded per clause.** |

**(ii)** the harness's **`stdio`** launch measured **`9` tools with `provident.load` ABSENT** (measurer: **the app-side unit's recorded class-(b) launches**, `docs/specs/unit-app-harness-readiness.md` `O-4`). **THE FRAMING, STATED PLAINLY AND NOT SOFTENED: IF the gap is REAL, the network-facing transport registers a tool set the default launch is contractually supposed to withhold — a surface-exposure difference between transports, which is a SECURITY-relevant finding, not a convenience one.** | **RECORDED READINGS, TWO MEASURERS, TWO TRANSPORTS — and the observation was NOT REPEATED, so it is `UNVERIFIED-BY-REPEAT` BY CONSTRUCTION.** **THE CONFOUNDER, RECORDED RATHER THAN HIDDEN (it is one of the two live explanations, and this row does NOT choose between them): the live profile is PERSISTENT (`/tmp/astrographer-live-P2Dl`) and the security config is a FILE IN THE APP'S `userData`** (`src/main/security-store.ts` takes a `path` whose doc-comment says *"usually in Electron userData"*, reads it with `readFileSync`, writes it with `writeFileSync`) — **so a profile whose config once enabled the `graph` group would ALSO read 12+ tools**, with no transport dependence at all. **The class-(b) launches spawn FRESH scratch profiles, which have no such file** — which is exactly why the comparison as it stands **cannot separate transport from profile state.** | **NOT DETERMINED, and this row does not pretend otherwise.** Two admissible explanations: **(a) TRANSPORT-DEPENDENT REGISTRATION/GATE** — the HTTP transport path registers (or gates) the tool groups differently from `stdio`; | Id | Severity | Symptom (observed) | Reproduction / evidence | Suspected root cause (upstream-owned) | Proposed fix shape (upstream) |

| **`G-8 OVERLAY-REMOVAL-IDENTITY-UNSATISFIABLE-AS-PRINTED`** | **LOW/MED** (FOUNDATION **spec wording** — a consumer-facing trap, not a behaviour bug: the module is not wrong and **must not be changed**) | **The foundation's spec prints an identity that is NOT SATISFIABLE AS A STRICT EQUATION on one of its two arms.** `../Provident-Electron/docs/specs/overlay.md` prints `removal === (value !== true)` — at **`§2.4` item 2**, repeated at **`§0A` note 2** (*"the record pins `removal === (value !== true)`"*) and in the **`§2.2(D)` semantics table**'s `removal` row, and **asserted on EVERY drive of the register**. But the module's **set arm returns `value` as the STRING `'true'`** (the declared closed two-member domain is `'true' \| false`), so on that arm `value !== true` is `true` while `removal` is `false`. **The honest consequence: the printed identity is unsatisfiable as written, and a consumer that asserts it as an equation gets a FALSE FAIL on correct behaviour — the trap is on the CONSUMER side of the boundary.** **The same file and the guide both record the defect as OWED, not as a repair** (the foundation's own `CURRENT STATE` item 11 clause (f) files it as *"the ONE CONTRACT-WORDING ITEM THIS PASS DID NOT REPAIR"* with *"the resolution is a dated annotation in a future pass"*; `../Provident-Electron/docs/guide/overlay.md` *Gotchas* warns *"Do not assert the printed identity as an equation"*). | **Read, not run (this pass has no shell; the readings are READS of the two trees):** (a) `../Provident-Electron/docs/specs/overlay.md` `§2.4` item 2 + `§0A` note 2 + the `§2.2(D)` `removal` row — the printed identity; and `§2.4` item 2 / `§0A` note 2's declared domain — **`value: 'true' \| false`**. (b) The vendored module **this repo** consumes, `src/shared/overlay.ts` — its set arm produces the record `{ value: 'true', removal: false }` and its removal arm `{ value: false, removal: true }` (the arms are re-stated in this repo's `docs/specs/pd-ui-6-adoption-dossier.md` §4 and in the foundation's own inert-route carry at `../Provident-Electron/docs/FORKER.md`). (c) **The falsifying reading is the printed equation itself on the set arm:** `value !== true` is `true` (the string `'true'` is not the boolean `true`), while `removal` is `false` → `false === true` is `false`. (d) **The declaration-only semantics are NOT in question:** the module performs no write, the removal case is DATA, and `'true'`/`'false'` as strings and `1` as a number are **NOT** the set case (`../Provident-Electron/docs/specs/overlay.md` §2.4 items 1–4; `../Provident-Electron/docs/guide/overlay.md` *Gotchas measured in this repo*). (e) **The defect is UNCHANGED by the foundation's docs-only commit `d7b98b574adc7fa63fbabda617eba2a753f52cb5`:** its new subsection states the arms CORRECTLY (`'true'` on the set arm, boolean `false` on the removal arm, discriminated by `removal` alone) **while the spec's printed identity was left as written** — so the guide now contradicts the spec in the one place the spec is the authority. (f) **The fork's own cross-check row:** `docs/specs/post-division-foundation-adoption-surface.md` §7 `G-5` records the same reading (*"the set arm's `value` is the **string** `'true'` — so the printed identity is not satisfiable as a strict equation on that arm"*). | **The spec's prose identity was written as a strict equation against the BOOLEAN `true` while the module's `value` domain was authored as the closed two-member `'true' \| false` — the wording and the bytes did not move together.** The **declared pair** (`{'true', false}` on the set arm; `{false, true}` on every other arm) is what `src/shared/overlay.ts` implements and what the contract's own §2.4 item 2 *table* pins; **the equation is the odd one out.** **This is upstream-owned and NOT a fork-side action, and it is NOT a reason to change the module** (changing `value` to the boolean would break the declared domain and every consumer that reads it). | **UPSTREAM-OWNED, AND IT IS A WORDING ANNOTATION — NEVER A MODULE CHANGE.** The fix shape is **one dated clause-level note in `../Provident-Electron/docs/specs/overlay.md`** that (1) records the identity's printed form as **superseded by the discriminator rule**, and (2) states the **satisfiable** form: the case is read from **`removal` alone** — `true` ⇒ `{ value: 'true', removal: false }`, anything else ⇒ `{ value: false, removal: true }` — **with the printed equation explicitly marked as not assertable** (it is already marked so on the guide side). **A module change, a domain change or a vendored-copy patch is NOT the fix and is forbidden here** (`AGENTS.md` item 7: the package/foundation is NEVER patched — a foundation defect is catalogued + handed off). **The matching handoff row is `docs/HANDOFF.md` §"OPEN handoff items" → `G-8 …`.** |

- **`U-EDIT-1-LIVE-6` — PARKED (a structural corpus precondition, not a defect).** *Park reason:* **no document in either corpus renders a table on its page-edit surface**, so the row ("a stored TABLE makes the commit REFUSE (typed) instead of flattening the stored `td/th/tr` nodes") could not be driven in the formal run — block `u_edit_1_live_package_table_limitation`, evidence `NO document in this store renders a table on its page-edit surface` (candidates `.live-corpus/alpha`, `defects` + up to 12 of `rag.list_documents`). *Recorded evidence that the refused behaviour IS what the spec pins* (the operator store, 63 documents incl. `defects`): rendered table census `{"tables":3,"trs":11,"tds":87}`, a typed edit + a REAL blur → `warning=true kind=decompose-failed class=commit-failed painted=true`, the text naming the table capability gap, `store read-back unchanged=true`, rendered tables after the refused commit still `3`. **That is not a PASS of the parked row.** *Revisit condition:* a corpus with a stored table on a page-edit surface. *Underlying upstream gap (handoff, never patched here):* `PROVIDENT-EDITABLE-TABLE-FLATTENED`.
- **`UF-STAGE-AT-7` — PARKED (a structurally non-exercisable seam, not a defect; RCA-11's own carve-out).** *Park reason + evidence:* **`mountTabs` has no bridge/DOM seam** — the sidebar bridge exposes no `mount*` key (`/mount/` keys `[]`), there is no window-level host object and no DOM affordance — and the seam's own doc-comment pins it **UNREACHABLE FROM PRODUCTION until `U-STATE-1e` lands** (`src/renderer/sidebar-panes.ts` `SidebarPanes.mountTabs`, doc-comment + body, read at this review). The multi-mount census (`A.1.2 destroyRoot`) is therefore unreachable from production; per RCA-11 this is a *structural* park **with the reason recorded**, not a parked-by-default unit — every reachable stage/`I2-R` state was exercised live instead (`UF-STAGE-AT-1`/`-3`/`-4` PASS). *Cross-refs:* live record §3.2/§4.6; `docs/specs/unit-stage-active-tab-display.md` §5.3.6; the defect row `MOUNT-TABS-CONTRADICTS-SINGLE-ACTIVE`.
- **`U-EDIT-1-LIVE-7` — the "ONE `edit.batch`" half is NOT live-measurable (a documented LIMITATION, never a pass claim).** The row's live half PASSES **on the STORE layer only**: a real typed edit + real blur changed the `rag.get_document` read-back, the marker is present in the store AND in the rendered DOM (they agree) and no warning was set. **The one-batch oracle's reachable journal channel is the wrong journal:** `provident.get_journal` is the **ENGINE Supervisor journal** (`src/main/mcp-server.ts` — the engine's undo/redo stacks), **NOT** `RagStore.journal()` / `handleRagJournalIpc` (the project journal the history sub-pane consumes); on this host the engine is `absent`, so the read is `{entries:0,undoDepth:0}` before and after the commit in one run and a `20 × destroy` delta in another (measurement-channel instability). The one-batch claim's only evidence therefore remains the **NODE** layer (`C1`: exactly one `applyBatch`, no `enqueue`). **Do not read `U-EDIT-1-LIVE-7`'s PASS as covering the batch count** (live record §4.2).

**PRODUCTION FIX OBSERVED LANDING DURING THIS REVIEW (recorded so the trackers do not carry a stale premise).** The `C9 U-EDIT-1` `M5` premise that `Runtime.extractContentRoots` and `applyContentReconcile`'s `destroyRoot` admit the SAME root set was **false on the tree the live battery ran against** (the extractor omitted `PAGE_COMMIT_WARNING_ID`, so the failure warning's reconcile-path attach was refused). It is **true again as of this review's closing re-read** (`src/renderer/runtime.ts` — the extractor now admits the class, with the omission and the refusal recorded in its own doc comment). The corrected premise + the evidence are recorded in `docs/specs/unit-stage-active-tab-display.md` §A.1.2 and `docs/specs/unit-u-edit-1-whole-page-editing.md` §3a `M5`; the node rows `W1`/`W2`/`W3` are owed a re-run by the owning unit.

PAGE_EDIT_SURFACE_ID` → the `#page-edit-surface` lookup → `editController.restoreCaret(pageSubject)`) instead of `:2006-2007`. **Line-number caveat (why the pins are given as symbols):** `src/renderer/sidebar-panes.ts` was being EDITED by the in-flight production-fix pass throughout this review (3 921 → 4 007 lines across the review's own reads), so every line pin here is a drift pin — the path + SYMBOL is the citation (`docs/specs/unit-u-edit-1-whole-page-editing.md` §11.10's anchor rule). **The audit-P8/P11 readings above** (`isDirty(doc-a)=true isDirty(t1)=false`, `pageEditSurfaceHandlerSubject=doc-a`, `restoreCaret(doc-a)=undefined`) **are pre-landing measurements on the audit's tree and must not be quoted as current**; consequences (3)/(5) were NOT re-measured by this review, and the row's OPEN/FIXED disposition is left to the owning unit's tracker pass — this review repairs citations and records drift only. **Live cross-ref (filed by this same review):** `U-EDIT-1-LIVE-8` — the page caret still does not reach the surface after a real re-derive in the running app (`insideSurface=false`), i.e. the caret clause's live half remains broken even with the tab-keyed subject landed. **Measured (audit P8):** `isDirty(doc-a)=true isDirty(t1)=false`, `pageEditSurfaceHandlerSubject=doc-a`. **Consequences: (1)** two tabs showing the SAME document silently share ONE dirty flag, ONE `pageCommitFailure` record and ONE caret slot — typing in one tab marks the other dirty and `isDirty('<tabId>')` is false throughout; **(2)** the page-caret restore is DEAD for every subject the host actually uses — `restoreCaret` gates on `opts.backRefs.has(subjectId)` (`src/renderer/edit-controller.ts:188-191`) while `backRefs` is a `Map<ragNodeId, string[]>` (`src/renderer/renderer.ts:940`), so a DOCUMENT id is rejected and the caret is deleted, never restored (audit P11: `restoreCaret(doc-a)=undefined`); **(3)** the dirty flag's blast radius is cross-tab — `anyDirty()` gates the GLOBAL rebuild (`edit-controller.ts:167-176`) and `rerenderAppGraph` (`sidebar-panes.ts:2976`), so one dirty page defers every other tab's re-derive; **(4)** no close/eviction seam reaches the host at all — `TabStrip.close` (`src/renderer/tab-strip.ts:157-161`) → `commit` → `onActiveChange`, and `renderer.ts:852-866` subscribes to `persist` + `onActiveChange` ONLY, so nothing tells the host "tab X closed" and no per-tab state can ever be evicted; **(5)** there is no store dimension anywhere in the host's per-page state (`sidebar-panes.ts:492-506` — `lastSnapshot`/`lastStore`/`lastDocHeads`/`lastCrosslinks`/`lastBacklinks`/`lastQueryResult`/`lastOperatorSettings`/`lastStoreListing`/`lastJournal` are host-global). **DOC DRIFT (same row, per AGENTS.md item 10d):** the tree's OWN doc-comments state the opposite (tab-keyed) contract — `sidebar-panes.ts:636-639` ("per-TAB … keyed by tab id"), `:3130-3133`, `:3142-3146` ("The `subject` is the tab id") and `edit-controller.ts:85-92` ("keyed by the PAGE subject id (the tab id …)"). | Open any document in TWO tabs (a doc-nav row → new tab, or `provident.focus {target:{kind:'document',documentId:'doc-a'}, newTab:true}`); edit in tab 1 through the production seams (`window.provident.sidebar.pageSurfaceInput()`, or an MCP `provident.dispatch` input on `page-edit-surface`): `editController.isDirty('doc-a')===true` while `isDirty('<tabId>')===false`, and tab 2 renders tab 1's uncommitted text with `pageCommitFailure` keyed `'doc-a'`. Caret: save a caret, trigger a re-derive → `restoreCaret(subject)` returns `undefined` for every subject the host passes. **Node-assertable NOW** (audit §4.1 `R3`/`R4`, exercised by the probe on the real modules with `installShim()`): mount a document tab + `pageSurfaceInput()` + mount a SECOND tab on the same document → the committed subject must be a TAB id and the second tab must be clean. | The host has **no tab identity**: `mountTab` records only "what is mounted" (`mountedStageKey = JSON.stringify(entry)`, `mountedDocumentIds` — `sidebar-panes.ts:719-750`) and keeps its notion of "the current page" in `_currentDocumentId` (`:451`, set only by `mountDocumentStage` at `:1019`, never cleared by a non-document mount). `TabStrip.onActiveChange` hands the host a `TabEntry` whose `id` is discarded, so the commit seam cannot even NAME the tab. This is I1's live violation: the audit's §0 verdict is `VIOLATED` for the state that exists, and the spec'd tab-scoped read cache (`docs/specs/unit-reads-pivot-tab-cache.md` — **landed spec, no code, no red set run**) does not exist to violate or satisfy (a repo-wide search for its symbols `residentKeys`/`TabCacheKey`/`CacheKind`/`MAX_RESIDENT_*`/`CacheMiss`/`CacheOverBound` returns 0 hits in `src/`, `tests/`, `dist/renderer/renderer.js`). | (a) Give the host an `activeTabId`/active-entry field, set in `mountTab` from `entry.id` for EVERY target kind and cleared for non-document targets; (b) key `markDirty`/`isDirty`/`commit`/`pageCommitFailure`/`saveCaret`/`restoreCaret` by that tab id; (c) fix `restoreCaret`'s guard so the PAGE subject is checked against the surface's DOCUMENT (or that document's backRefs root), never the tab id against the ragId-keyed map; (d) add the missing close/eviction notification (`TabStrip` → host) so a closed tab's page state is dropped. | **STAGE-RACE-ASYNC-MOUNT (2026-09-22 — filed by the tab/page-ownership audit pass; the I2 live violation, V1)** | **high (HOST — assembled/app: the renderer host's async mount ordering) — OPEN (NEW)** | **A pending async search mount OVERWRITES a newer active tab's stage.** With a search mount in flight, switching to (or opening) a DOCUMENT tab leaves `#zone:main` showing the **search** view: the document body AND the page-edit surface are both gone, so the operator cannot edit the document they just opened. **Measured (audit P3 + P14, deterministic with a 40 ms query delay):** `P3 pre-resolve :: BETA=true search-body=false surface=doc-b` → `P3 post-resolve :: BETA=false search-body=true input=true surface=(no surface)` while the active tab is `t2/doc-b`; and through the HOST-4 result-click path `P14 post-resolve :: active={"kind":"document","documentId":"doc-b"} BETA=false search-body=true surface=(no surface)`. `mountTab` for a `search` target sets `mountedStageKey` and calls `void this.mountSearchStage(entry)` (`src/renderer/sidebar-panes.ts:734-739`); `mountSearchStage` (`:1026-1048`) `await`s `this.bridge.rag.query(...)` (`:1033`) and then applies `applyStageBody(searchTabContent(entry, …))` (`:1047`) with **no re-check that it is still the active mount** — `mountedStageKey` (`:725`) only suppresses an IDENTICAL entry, so a newer mount is never protected from an older completion; a document/landing body, by contrast, is applied SYNCHRONOUSLY (`:731`/`:749`). The race window is the FULL `rag.query` IPC round trip — live this is engine latency, so it is a wide window, not a corner case. | Boot with ≥1 document; `expandSearchTab('alpha')` from the search pane (`#pane-search-expand-tab` → `SEARCH_EXPAND_TAB_BODY`, `src/renderer/pane-graph.ts:950-955` → `tab-strip.ts:172-179` → `commit` → `onActiveChange` → `mountTab`); INSIDE the query round trip open a document tab (`openDocumentTab('doc-b')`, or a real click on a search-result row / a doc-nav row) → read `#zone:main`: `#stage-search-tab` + `#search-tab-input` present, `[data-edit-surface]` absent, while `.tab.is-active[data-tab-id]` names the DOCUMENT tab. **Node assertion (audit §4.1 `R1`):** with a slow stub `bridge.rag.query`, `mountTab(searchTab)` then `mountTab(docTab('doc-b'))` → after the query settles `innerHTML` must still contain the doc-b body and must NOT contain `stage-search-tab`/`search-tab-input` (today: inverted). | `SidebarPanes.mountSearchStage` has **no mount-generation guard**: the apply at `:1046-1047` is unconditional after the await, and the host has no "active entry" identity to compare against (no `activeTabId` field — the same missing seam `PAGE-STATE-NOT-TAB-OWNED` needs). **FORK-SIDE ENGINE ADOPTION DEFECTS FILED 2026-09-28 (the `W0` dossier-and-decision pass; gate findings `A-9`+`X-7`; the two rows the ENGINE-side inventory `docs/specs/post-division-engine-offload-inventory.md` §4 `PD-ENG-13`/§3 item 5 and `PD-ENG-12` recorded and that NO tracker row carried).** **WHAT THEY ARE:** two landed engine wire additions are **invisible to, or silently mis-decoded by, this repo's clients** — the engine's **versioned mutating payload** (accepted as if unversioned, **its commit token discarded**) and the engine's new **`durability` axis** on the `HealthReport` (**decoded-and-dropped**). **OWNER + INDEPENDENCE, stated so neither is mis-scoped:** both are **HOST-side (`src/**`) adoption defects owned by THIS repo** (they are NOT `provident-ssr` package defects and NOT handoffs — `AGENTS.md` item 7), they are **INDEPENDENT of the P2 authority-switch chain** (the engine's fold and its `durability` axis have **landed**; nothing upstream is waited on), and each is owed to the **six-item engine ADDITIVE adoption set** the `docs/specs/post-division-rebuild-proposal.md` §4.3 / §4.7 Phase-2 records — **additive work that eliminates nothing**. **LAYER (RCA-12): VERIFIED-BY-READ of this repo's `src/**` + the engine's landed sources read from the sibling tree; no suite, no build and no app was run by the filing pass, so nothing here is GREEN at any layer.**

| Id | Severity | Symptom (observed) | Reproduction / evidence | Suspected root cause | Proposed fix shape |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **`FORK-ENGINE-CRUD-VERSION-TOKEN-DISCARDED`** | **MEDIUM** (HOST — `src/main/`, the CRUD client; silent-drift hazard, not a crash) | **The engine's folded mutating payload is READ AS UNVERSIONED and its commit token is THROWN AWAY.** The engine emits `{method, resultVersion: 2, result, commitToken}` for its **7** mutating document-CRUD methods; this repo's `decodeCrudResponse` (`src/main/engine-crud-rag-store.ts`) accepts exactly one of `result`/`error` and **never checks `resultVersion`**, so a future fold to `resultVersion: 3` would be **silently accepted as if it were the version the client understands**, and the `commitToken` — the engine's restore-semantics handle (`ENGINE-COMMIT-TOKEN`; | **`FORK-ENGINE-HEALTH-DURABILITY-AXIS-DROPPED`** | **MEDIUM** (HOST — `src/main/`, the retrieval/health client; operator-visible honesty hazard) | **The engine's `durability` axis is decoded-and-DISCARDED, so this app cannot tell a durable engine from an in-memory one.** The engine's `HealthReport` now carries **7** top-level keys — the six pre-`D-D1` keys **plus `durability`** (`"durable" | "in-memory" | "disabled"`, a read-time projection) — while this repo's `HealthReport` type (`src/main/engine-rag-store.ts`) has **no such field** and `decodeHealthReport` is **total over its known keys**, so the value is silently dropped at the decode boundary. **Consequence:** `gnosis.status`, the `gnosis-status` pane and `engine-crud-rag-store.ts`'s READY gate all read a health report **with the durability signal removed** — the exact class `D-D1`'s `R-L5` live row exists to make observable (`chmod 0555` ⇒ `durability:"disabled"`; | **PROVIDENT-EDITABLE-TABLE-FLATTENED (2026-09-22 — filed from `C9 U-EDIT-1`'s adapter build; re-verified against `dist/html-to-tree.js` before filing; the RUNTIME half of `PROVIDENT-EDITABLE-NO-TABLE-ELEMENTS`)** | **medium (PACKAGE `provident-editable@0.2.0` — the PURE conversion layer: the block-tag set) — OPEN (handoff; NEVER patched here)** | **`BLOCK_TAGS` carries no `table`/`thead`/`tr`/`td`/`th`, and the converter's fall-through branch FLATTENS any element it does not know** — the element is skipped and its text is folded into the surrounding run — so a table's structure is silently gone from the returned tree, with **no throw, no warning and no dropped-content report**. The two halves must be fixed together: adding a type member without the `BLOCK_TAGS` entry still flattens. | **Upstream-owned (proposed fix shape, never patched here):** (a) add the table family to `ProvidentNodeType` **and** to the converter's block table so a table round-trips as structure (the type-union half is `PROVIDENT-EDITABLE-NO-TABLE-ELEMENTS`); (b) make the unknown-element branch **non-silent** — return a dropped/flattened-tag report (result field or an `opts` hook) instead of folding the content into the run, so a consumer can refuse deterministically instead of inferring. **Do NOT patch `node_modules/provident-editable/` or `../Provident-Editable/`** (`AGENTS.md` items 1/7). | **PROVIDENT-EDITABLE-DEPTH-CAP-SILENT-DROP (2026-09-22 — filed from `C9 U-EDIT-1`'s adapter build; re-verified against `dist/html-to-tree.js` + `dist/diff.js` before filing)** | **low/medium (PACKAGE `provident-editable@0.2.0` — the PURE conversion layer: the private recursion guard) — OPEN (handoff; NEVER patched here)** | **The converter's depth cap DROPS content past `MAX_DEPTH` with no report**, and the cap is a PRIVATE constant a consumer cannot import. `MAX_DEPTH = 512` (`dist/html-to-tree.js:9`); past it the converter returns empty nodes / empty text silently — `process()` returns for `d > MAX_DEPTH` (`:328-330`), `buildBlock` returns `{ id: '', type: tag, content: '', build: '' }` (`:439-441`), `extractPreText` returns `''` (`:462-464`), `buildInline` returns `{ blankDrop: false }` (an inline and its text vanish, `:486-488`), and `isPlainLeaf` stops propagating at `depth >= MAX_DEPTH` (`:429-433`). The doc-comment states the contract deliberately (*"Content at/below this depth is truncated/dropped deterministically … The conversion returns a tree, never throws"*, `:3-9`) — determinism was paired with silence, so a page deeper than the cap decodes to a **valid-looking but truncated tree with no signal**. **Consequence for this repo:** the adapter had to mirror the boundary it cannot import and REFUSE a page that crosses it — `src/main/page-diff.ts:246-253` (`const PACKAGE_MAX_DEPTH = 512`, whose own comment calls it *"the package's own pinned recursion guard"*) + `:414-419` (the refusal: *"nests beyond the decomposer's depth guard (512) — the content past it would be dropped silently"*), pinned by `docs/specs/unit-u-edit-1-whole-page-editing.md` §7's `P-TP-1` row. | Read the installed dist at the lines above, then check the public surface: `grep -r MAX_DEPTH node_modules/provident-editable/dist` matches **only** `html-to-tree.js:9/329/335/432/439/463/487` and `diff.js:54/455` — **no `.d.ts` and no barrel export** (`dist/index.d.ts` exports 3 values + 5 types; `dist/html-to-tree.d.ts` exports only `htmlToTree`), so the cap is unreachable from a consumer. **Repro (this repo's adapter):** a page nested past the mirrored cap → `decodePage` → `{ ok: false, kind: 'decompose-failed', message: "…nests beyond the decomposer's depth guard (512) — the content past it would be dropped silently" }`; a page one level inside it decodes normally, with no evidence that the boundary is the package's. **The mirror is UNVERIFIED BY CONSTRUCTION:** a repo-wide search for `PACKAGE_MAX_DEPTH`/`maxNestingDepth` matches only `src/main/page-diff.ts` (no test pins the boundary), and the adapter's counting convention (outermost element = depth 1, `:258-276`) is not the package's internal `depth` convention (`buildTopLevel` starts blocks at 1, `:540-579`; `buildChildren` at the parent's depth, `:296-418`) — so the two boundaries can be one level apart with neither side noticing. | The guard was written for stack safety (§5.9 fail-state 7 / `HOST-DIFF-4`: *"deeply nested HTML can NEVER throw a stack overflow"*), and it chose silence; the constant was also kept private, so no consumer can assert the same boundary rather than copy it. **A second, DISTINCT private cap exists in the diff:** `dist/diff.js:54` (`MAX_DEPTH = 512`) with `:447-455`'s traversal bound — that one is DOCUMENTED and reported deterministically (an at-bound pair becomes a subtree-granular `remove` + `add`, never a silent field drop), which is exactly the observability the converter's cap lacks; the two same-valued constants are unrelated and neither is exported. | **Upstream-owned (proposed fix shape, never patched here):** (a) EXPORT the cap (e.g. `export const MAX_DEPTH`, or a `ConvertOptions` cap with a documented default) so a consumer can pin the same boundary instead of mirroring a literal; and (b) REPORT the drop — a result-side field (e.g. `{ root, dropped: { depth, count } }`) or an `opts` callback — instead of a silent empty subtree, so a truncated decode can be refused deterministically. Until both exist this repo mirrors the literal, refuses, and must re-verify the mirror on every `provident-editable` bump. **Do NOT patch `node_modules/provident-editable/` or `../Provident-Editable/`** (`AGENTS.md` items 1/7). Handoff row: `docs/HANDOFF.md` → **`PROVIDENT-EDITABLE-DEPTH-CAP-SILENT-DROP`**. |

| **HEAVY-OPS-FREEZE-THE-PAGE (2026-09-16, live — user report: "document uploading and other heavy JS code stops the page from responding to basic inputs like page resize")** | **high (HOST + ARCHITECTURE) — OPEN (NEW)** | Ordinary document interactions freeze the UI: opening a document from the doc-nav makes the page unresponsive for ~1s (two **439 ms** main-thread long tasks), and expanding a doc-nav folder alone costs a **505 ms** long task. The engine/MCP side is NOT the blocker (queries/imports are main-process); the RENDERER's synchronous re-derivation is. | (a) Stop re-deriving the whole app graph for a local UI change: a doc-nav folder toggle must re-render ONLY the doc-nav pane body (a scoped pane reconcile), a document open must mount only the stage payload, and a pane visibility/size change must not touch the document graph at all; (b) move anything O(store) off the interaction path (virtualize the doc-nav list, cap the rendered rows, chunk/async the reconcile and yield to the event loop so a resize is never starved); (c) keep the long-task budget asserted live (owed row: no pane/doc interaction produces a >100 ms task). NOTE the user's architectural point recorded below (`ARCH-GNOSIS-OFFLOAD`). |
| **CANVAS-DIMENSIONS-JS-DRIVEN (2026-09-16, live — user report: "the dimensions of the canvas/frame should be basic CSS and JS should not be involved in adjusting to a window resize at all")** | **medium (HOST) — OPEN (NEW)** | The canvas/frame dimensions are written by JS as inline CSS custom properties on `<html>` (`--zone-left-size: 220px; --zone-right-size: 220px; --zone-header-size: 48px; --zone-footer-size: 48px; --stage-weight: 640fr; --top-bar-size: 36px`), and the stage track is authored as a `fr` value derived from a JS "size" number — so layout geometry is JS-owned even though a window resize should be pure CSS. | Live: `document.documentElement.getAttribute('style')` = the variable list above; `#wiki-root` computed `grid-template-columns: 220px 1583.91px 0px` and `grid-template-rows: 0px 12698.7px 0px`; `--stage-weight: 640fr` is the app's own serialization (`layout-state.ts:221/282`) of the stage "size" number. Idle resize latency is fine (6 ms) because the browser re-lays-out from the variables, but every layout STATE write re-serializes the same variables from JS (each write costs a style recalc on the huge page), and the sizing model is a JS number rather than CSS. | The layout model keeps zone sizes + a stage "size" in `LayoutState` and projects them into CSS custom properties on every change (`layoutCssVars`, `layout-state.ts:217-222`). Owed live row: after a resize, geometry follows with no JS layout write and no layout-state commit. |

| **VECTOR-IMPORT-NO-BODY-VECTORS (2026-09-15, live — ollama/embeddinggemma vector-mode pass)** | **high (HOST) — OPEN (NEW)** | In vector mode, documents brought in by an import are NOT retrievable by their BODY text until each node is edited once: `rag.query` returns `[]` for body phrases right after the import, while a single `edit.set_content` on a node makes that node (and only it) retrievable. The engine forwards that list verbatim to the embedder hook (`src/main/retrieval.ts` `onStoreChanged`), and `src/main/embeddings.ts` `addToVectorIndex` embeds `node.content` — a document root's `content` is `''`, so it is recorded `skipped: 'empty'` and the body nodes are never embedded. (The LEXICAL index escapes this by accident: `nodeText()` = `content + children[].content`, so the root's inline children carry the text into BM25.)  **⟨ANNOTATED `2026-10-05` BY THE GATE-5 PASS OF `U-MOCK-CORPUS-FIXTURE-SETS` (`RCA-8(c)`, ANNOTATE-BESIDE — the sentence above is KEPT as its pass's reading): THE LEXICAL INDEX DOES *NOT* ESCAPE THIS. MEASURED, in the driver's default LEXICAL mode (not vector mode), right after an `edit.import_markdown` of a fixture document: `rag.query` for the document's own term returns **`0 hit(s)`** — `9 nodes, 3 of them carrying the term` — because the import's reconcile is handed **document ROOT ids alone** and `parseMarkdown` authors every document root with **EMPTY `content`** (`makeNode(documentId,'div','')`) while the body lives in separate `:section:`/`:p:` nodes whose text `nodeText(root) = content + children[].content` does NOT reach (the section node's own `content` is empty; the text is one level deeper). CONSEQUENCE FOR THIS ROW: the defect is **engine-mode-independent** — a document imported and never edited is unretrievable in LEXICAL mode too — so the fix shape below must cover BOTH indexes, and the *'by accident'* parenthetical is `SUPERSEDED` in that half. THE UNIT'S OWN WORKAROUND IS NOT AN APP FIX AND DOES NOT CLOSE THIS ROW: `U-MOCK-CORPUS-FIXTURE-SETS` writes the imported documents' authored text onto their ROOT nodes through the app's existing node-level `edit.set_content` op (its own materialisation route — `docs/specs/unit-mock-corpus-fixture-sets.md` `§22.2`), which makes the query function for ITS fixture by putting text where the reconcile actually looks, and leaves every other import path unretrievable. **THIS ROW STAYS OPEN (HOST).** || Pass the imported node ids (not just the document roots) to the reconcile — e.g. widen `importMarkdownCorpus`'s result (or have the caller walk `store.getNode(docId).ownedNodeIds` + the doc-child/next-section closure) so every imported CONTENT node reaches `onStoreChanged`; alternatively make `addToVectorIndex` fall back to `nodeText(node)` (content + inline children) the way the lexical index does, since a section/div root legitimately carries its text in children. Cover with a vector-mode import test (import → query the body phrase → expect ≥1 result) — currently NO test covers import→vector retrieval (the W2/W5 suites cover boot-build and edit-reconcile only). |

Add a server-level test that the four modes produce different traces/results on a populated graph. Recorded in `docs/HANDOFF.md`. |

the click-probe's event chain now names the ROW's own compiled node (`pointerdown/mouseup/click = preempt-node-* in#pane-*`) instead of retargeting to `.pane-frame[data-pane-id]`; and the header toggle that the old capture had also broken works again (`uf_panes_1`: doc-nav body `li=66`/h=9681px → real click → `is-collapsed=true`, `li=0`, h=37px header-only → second real click → `li=66`, h=9681px, same pane root `#pane-doc-nav`). See `docs/specs/user-flow-audit-coverage-2026-09-15.md` §4 F-1. | doc-nav body `li=66`/h=9681px → real click → `is-collapsed=true`, `li=0`, h=37px header-only → second real click → `li=66`, h=9681px, same pane root `#pane-doc-nav`). Evidence the running bundle carries the fix: `dist/renderer/renderer.js` contains `.gutter[data-zone], .pane-collapse-toggle` and `PANE_DRAG_CAPTURE_THRESHOLD`. ## SHELVED

| # | Severity | Symptom | Reproduction | Suspected root cause | Proposed fix shape (upstream-owned) | Shelved reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **ENG-GAP-1** | a-big (shelved) | The `MarkdownAdapter` drops `data:*` props including the opt-in `data-node-id` (D7), so the agent-facing markdown output carries no element→node identity. | Render a graph with `renderOptions.nodeIdAttribute: true`, then re-emit through a fresh `MarkdownAdapter`; the markdown output has no node identity. | `MarkdownAdapter` (adapters.md §4.7 D7) drops `on:*` and `data:*` props; only `href`/`title`/`src`/`alt` are rendered. | A handoff feature request: a markdown-adapter option to preserve node identity (or a node-id-preserving markdown variant). | **Shelved 2026-08-26 (user ruling).** (1) Markdown is EXPORT-ONLY — no flow edits markdown output as input, so node identity in the export has no consumer today. (2) Comment IDs are fragile: an external editor could delete/alter them, corrupting the mapping. (3) The future markdown-parsing-to-storage feature will rely on **text-match diffing** instead of node-identity comments (see `docs/pending.md`). Revisit only if a markdown-as-input flow becomes supported. |

| **HOST-SHELL-WIRING-DEAD-SELECTORS (+H2–H5)** | high (HOST) — **FIXED (2026-09-13, U-SHELL-N7)** | The `installShellPointers` shell pointer-wiring attached pointers only to elements found at INSTALL time — `document.querySelectorAll('.gutter[data-zone]')` / `.pane-frame[data-pane-id]` matched **NO authored DOM** (the frame root had no `data-pane-id`; index.html authored only `.gutter` CSS), so C4/C7/C11/C12 were unreachable live and the §2.8 source-pin was a false green. Related: HOST-2 (per-gesture `pointermove/up/cancel/dblclick` never removed → duplicate/stale commits), HOST-3 (`isInteractiveControl` no ancestor climb → a control's nested child mis-starts a drag, F9), HOST-4 (a reveal-driven re-mount detaches the captured element mid-gesture), HOST-5 (`toZoneBounds` coerces an unknown `[data-zone]` → `'left'`, fails open). | With the app rendered, C7/C4 gestures did nothing — no `startGutter`/`startPaneDrag` call, no `setLayout` write, the `.gutter` CSS was dead. | The wiring queried elements at install (before the graph rendered), registered per-gesture listeners that never teardown, inspected only the deepest node, captured onto an element a re-mount detaches, and coerced malformed zones into a real `left` drop target. | **FIXED (host, U-SHELL-N7, 2026-09-13):** `installShellPointers` (now a NAMED export of `src/renderer/renderer.ts`) is reworked as a document-level DELEGATED wiring — ONE delegated `pointerdown` resolved per event via `e.target.closest('.gutter[data-zone], .pane-frame[data-pane-id]')` (HOST-1/HOST-4); the per-gesture move/up/cancel registered exactly once + torn down at gesture end (HOST-2); ancestor-climb interactive guard incl. `select`/`textarea` + WRAPPER `label`/`fieldset`/`[contenteditable]` (HOST-3 + ADV5); fail-closed `isLayoutZoneNameValue` zone skip + gutter exclusion (HOST-5). Re-audit ADV1..5 also landed: permanent document-delegated `dblclick` reset; `pointerId`-scoped move/up/cancel + `lostpointercapture` teardown; cross-kind supersede revert; idempotent install; WRAPPER controls. The four `.gutter[data-zone][data-axis]` elements authored in `index.html`; the frame `data-pane-id` in `pane-graph.ts`; dom-shim extended (`innerHTML`/`outerHTML` getters + a delegated pointer dispatch + `closest`/`setRect`/`setPointerCapture`). Regressions: `archive/tests/2026-10-04-unit-u-shell-shell-wiring-adversarial.test.ts` 14 (HOST-1..5 + ADV1..5) + `archive/tests/2026-10-04-unit-u-shell-shell-wiring-pbt-generators.test.ts` 5. Trio green (**4527 pass / 58 skip**). Spec `unit-u-shell-shell-wiring.md` §3a/§3b. |
| **U-SHELL-9b-F1 (buildOwnersMap prototype-key throw)** | high (HOST) — **FIXED (2026-09-13, PBT audit)** | `buildOwnersMap` THREW on an edge whose `target` is an Object.prototype key — `buildOwnersMap([{ target: '__proto__', documentIds: ['A'] }])` / `'constructor'` → `TypeError: list.includes is not a function`. Surfaced by the U-SHELL-9b read-only PBT audit (F1) — the property generator's `target ∈ {'X',''}` could never sample it. | Drive `buildOwnersMap` with an edge object whose `target` is `'__proto__'`/`'constructor'`/`'toString'`/`'hasOwnProperty'`/`'valueOf'` and a non-empty `documentIds`. | `const owners = {}` is a plain object; `owners['__proto__']`/`owners['constructor']` resolve to the INHERITED non-array prototype value, so `?? ` never initializes and `.includes` throws — a totality violation of the register's "malformed edge is ignored / never throws / no prototype pollution" promise. | **FIXED (host, 2026-09-13):** `buildOwnersMap` now builds on a **null-prototype map** (`Object.create(null)`): a prototype-key `target` is an ordinary OWN key (never inherited, never pollutes, `.includes` never throws). Surfaced by the U-STATE-1e read-only PBT audit (W2) — the global `consumed` suppression, intended to leave B's UNCHANGED shared root kept (state 6), also suppressed B's real same-broadcast edit. | Broadcast: A forks `X→X′` (payload names `X`,`X′`) while B's `previous [rag-X]` → `next [rag-X edited]` (shape changed, subtree ids identical) in the same `change.nodeIds`. | `reconcileDocumentRoots` dropped the consumed fork ids from `effectiveChanged` GLOBALLY, so B's `payloadHit` is false and shape is only consulted when `useFallback||isPane` (false for B's content root) → B's edited X → `kept`. | **FIXED (host, 2026-09-13):** a root whose subtree carries a fork-consumed id (`carriesConsumedId`) now consults the SHAPE (`useShape`), so a genuine sibling edit flips to `replaced` while the pure fork-echo (unchanged shape) stays `kept` (state 6 preserved). Regression: `archive/tests/2026-10-04-props-reconcile-1e.test.ts` (`strat:nroot-fork-consumed-edit` + `strat:nroot-fork-echo-kept`). Trio green (195 files / 4469 pass + 58 skip). | **HOST-GUI-DOCS-PANE-DEADLOCK** | medium (HOST — **live-verified FIXED 2026-09-11**) | The `gnosis-documents` GUI pane was STUCK in whole-pane `data-gnosis-state="unavailable"` and rendered 0 clickable wiki `<li>` items / 0 doc `<li>` items, even after the `gnosis.wiki.list` bridge resolved (the `gnosis-wikis` pane rendered the same wiki list fine). A chicken-and-egg deadlock in the PURE helper `gnosisDocumentsContent` (`src/renderer/pane-graph.ts`): it returned the whole `unavailable` branch whenever `state.documents == null`, but the clickable wiki `<li>` items — the ONLY path that triggers `gnosis.document.list` (which sets `documents`) — were rendered only in the NON-unavailable branch. So with `documents` null the wiki selector never rendered → the pane could never leave `unavailable`. The module greens could not see it (they feed BOTH `wikis` AND `documents` directly to the pure helper). | Re-driven live on the relaunched app (`--remote-debugging-port=9222`): prior run click `gnosis-documents-refresh` → `pane-gnosis-documents` STILL `data-gnosis-state="unavailable"` with 0 `li[data-wiki-id]`; `pane-gnosis-wikis` rendered all wiki names; `[data-gnosis-pane="status"]` rendered `Ready`. 2026-09-11. | `gnosisDocumentsContent` gated the entire pane on `state.documents != null`; the wiki `<li>` selector (which would set `documents`) lived BELOW that gate, so it was unreachable while `documents` was null. The `gnosis-wikis` pane self-populates on `boot()` independent of document state, so only the documents pane deadlocked. | **FIXED (host, 2026-09-11):** `gnosisDocumentsContent` re-derived — the wiki selector renders whenever `state.wikis != null` (independent of `documents`); the Documents section shows `data-gnosis-docstate="empty"` ("No documents — select a wiki") when `documents` is null/empty. **Live-verified 2026-09-11 on the fresh relaunch (rebuilt `dist/`):** after a fresh `gnosis-documents-refresh` click the pane renders the wiki selector (10 `<li data-wiki-id>` live wiki names) AND the `data-gnosis-docstate="empty"` empty state — NO whole-pane `data-gnosis-state="unavailable"`, no `unavailable` anywhere; `pane-gnosis-wikis` (10 wikis) + `pane-gnosis-status` (Ready) unaffected. Module contract green: unit-a2 (91) + props-a2 (8) = 99/99. The full G29 doc-population slice (select a wiki → doc-19 `<li>` renders) remains PARKED in the A2 battery pending a provident-capable UI-drive (CDP-probe can't dispatch the provident handler-`select-wiki` binding) — not a defect. |

| **HOST-READ-GET-WITH-BODY** (HOST-GET-WITH-BODY-SSE-CRUD) | medium (HOST — **live-verified FIXED 2026-09-11**) | The document-CRUD **read** methods issued a **GET-with-a-body** using `globalThis.fetch`; under Node/undici a GET with a body is rejected (`TypeError: Request with GET/HEAD method cannot have body`), so `gnosis.wiki.list`/`get`, `gnosis.document.get`/`list` failed at the transport layer. The app itself (Electron running the same undici fetch) reproduced it. | Live `tools/call` on the read tools over the running app → the GET-body TypeError. | `createEngineFetch` returned `globalThis.fetch`, which rejects GET-with-body. | **FIXED (host, 2026-09-11):** `createCrudFetch()` added in `src/main/engine-transport.ts` (uses undici `request()` — GET-with-body-capable, applies TLS/dispatcher) and wired as the CRUD client default (`src/main/engine-crud-rag-store.ts` `opts.fetch ?? createCrudFetch`). TDD: red set `archive/tests/2026-10-04-engine-crud-real-transport.test.ts` (GET-with-body reads failed) → green 2/2; existing A1 suite 60/60 still green. **Live-verified 2026-09-11 on the relaunched app:** `wiki.list`, `wiki.get`, `document.list`, `document.get` all resolve (no TypeError). NOTE: `document.list` on a populated wiki previously still surfaced the separate `HOST-CRUD-LIST-SUMMARY-DECODE` decode finding — **now FIXED + live-verified 2026-09-11** (see its FIXED row below). |
| **HOST-CRUD-DELETE-RESULT-SERIALIZATION** | medium (HOST — **live-verified FIXED 2026-09-11**) | `gnosis.document.delete` (a successful engine delete returning the wire `void`/`null`) failed at the MCP boundary with `MCP error -32602: Invalid tools/call result` — a result-SERIALIZATION error, not an engine error. | `tools/call gnosis.document.delete` → `-32602`. | The A2 `handleGnosisTool` delete case returned `undefined`, which `text(undefined)` cannot serialize to a valid tool result. | **FIXED (host, 2026-09-11):** the `gnosis.document.delete` case in `src/main/mcp-server.ts` now `await engineCrud.deleteDocument(...)` then `return null` (the wire `result:null`). Pinned tests updated to the null contract (`unit-a2…` §5.8-7 80/80; `blind-unit-a2…` G7 49/49). **Live-verified 2026-09-11 on the relaunched app:** `document.delete` returns the wire result `null`, no `-32602`. |
| **HOST-CRUD-LIST-SUMMARY-DECODE** | medium (HOST — **live-verified FIXED 2026-09-11**; was OPEN/live-confirmed) | `gnosis.document.list` on a wiki with >=1 document previously returned the engine error `malformed document` instead of the typed `DocumentList {items,total,page,pageSize}`. The read transport fix (HOST-GET-WITH-BODY-SSE-CRUD) let the call through for the first time; the deterministic suite could not see it (the mock feeds a FULL document via `DOC_WIRE`). | `tools/call gnosis.document.list {wikiId:<wiki-with-a-doc>}` over the live app (engine Ready) previously → `malformed document`. Reproduced on a fresh wiki immediately after `document.create`. The raw engine `GET /documents` wire is CORRECT: `{items:[{document_id,wiki_id,title,state,revision,updated_at}],total,page,page_size}` — a lightweight `DocumentSummary` per Gnosis §4.1.3. | The A1/A2 pinned contract declared `DocumentList.items: Document[]` (full docs) and `decodeDocumentList` mapped every item through the strict `decodeDocument`, which requires `graph`/`tags`/`created_at`/`author`-key present — fields the engine's `DocumentSummary` does not carry → `malformed document`. The engine is CORRECT (it returns a documented summary); the host over-required. | **FIXED (host, 2026-09-11):** the A1 `DocumentList` contract was re-derived to `items: DocumentSummary[]` and the host decodes the summary list via the lenient `decodeDocumentSummary` (six fields, snake+camel; tolerates a full `Document` item / projects it; a malformed summary → `EngineError('malformed document')` 502). **Live-verified 2026-09-11 on the fresh relaunch:** `gnosis.document.list` on a populated wiki resolves to the typed `DocumentList` (`items` six-field `DocumentSummary[]` — `documentId`/`wikiId`/`title`/`state`/`revision`/`updatedAt`, no full-doc leakage — `total:1, page:1, pageSize:20`; no `malformed document`, no `-32602`); on an empty wiki → `items:[], total:0, page:1, pageSize:20`. See  `archive/greens/2026-09-21-unit-a1-crud-list-summary-decode-greens.md` (historical; archived 2026-09-21; successor: the owning unit spec + the DONE row carrying the verification; archived basename unit-a1-crud-list-summary-decode-greens.md) §LIVE-LOCATION. |
| **HOST-GN-H11** | minor (documented limitation) | The loopback check (`assertLoopback`, INTERNAL in `src/main/engine-rag-store.ts`) accepts `localhost` unconditionally — it does not resolve the hostname to confirm it maps to a loopback address. | `createEngineRagStore({ baseUrl: 'http://localhost:8080' })` constructs without throwing (accepted per §5.8-14 / H11). | The factory is SYNCHRONOUS (no network I/O at construction — a pinned contract), so DNS resolution is not feasible in the sync factory. | **PARTIAL — DOCUMENTED LIMITATION (no code change):** the check now recognizes IPv4-mapped IPv6 (`::ffff:127.0.0.1`/`::ffff:7f00:1`); `localhost` remains accepted as the loopback alias per §5.8-14. A non-loopback address still throws at construction (`engine rag store: baseUrl must be loopback`). Found in the Unit GN adversarial pass (RCA-3, finding H11); regression-tested. **No package/upstream finding** — the Unit GN adversarial pass confirmed NO defect in the `provident-ssr` package or the Gnosis wire contract (spec §3b: NONE). |
| **HOST-LIVE-ZOD-SEAM** | medium | The SDK zod input-schema seam pre-empts the handler's documented M1/M2/documentId fail messages, so the batteries/specs overstate what is MCP-observable. Three manifestations (live-testing findings F2/F3/F4, 2026-09-08): **(F2)** a non-string `store` (`null`/`5`/`true`/`{}`/`[]`) is rejected by the `store: z.string().optional()` schema with `-32602 … expected string, received … at store` BEFORE the handler — only `''` reaches `resolveStoreArg` and yields M1 `store must be a non-empty string` (spec §5.1 M1 + battery §3.3 S19 overstate); **(F3)** an unknown `store` with a MISSING required arg (e.g. `create_node {store:'nope'}` without `type`/`content`) is rejected by the schema with `-32602` before the handler's store-resolution — M2 `unknown store` fires only when the call passes the zod schema (battery §3.3 S21 "every time" overstates); **(F4)** `rag.get_document {}` (missing `documentId`) is rejected by the required `documentId: z.string()` schema with `-32602 … expected string, received undefined at documentId` — only `''` reaches the handler's `documentId required` (V2 battery §3.3 C4 overstates). | Live MCP calls (2026-09-08 live-testing session, `archive/live-testing-findings/2026-09-08-findings.md`) with a non-string `store`, an unknown `store` + missing required args, and `rag.get_document {}`; each returns the SDK `-32602` error instead of the documented handler message. | The MCP SDK validates each tool's `inputSchema` (zod) BEFORE the handler runs; the schemas are type-strict (`store: z.string().optional()`, `documentId: z.string()` required), so type-invalid / missing-required-arg calls never reach the handler's documented fail messages. The spec's M1/M2/documentId contracts are correct at the PURE resolver/handler level (direct calls in tests) but overstate what the MCP surface observes. | Doc/battery erratum (NO code change — the SDK seam behavior is sound and matches the house "schema validates type, handler validates value" topK pattern): amend spec §5.1 M1 + §5.3 R6/R7 + battery §3.3 S19/S21 + V2 battery §3.3 C4 to note that non-string `store` / missing-required-arg / missing-`documentId` calls are rejected at the SDK zod seam with `-32602`, and only `''` reaches the handler's M1/M2/documentId messages. |
| **HOST-LIVE-UX-SETUP** | medium | The Unit X live-pending battery's setup is not buildable as written: it seeds its provenance graph with `edit.create_node {type:'content'|'fact'|'reference'}` (battery §2), but `edit.create_node` is a STRUCTURAL op (validates `type` against `RAG_NODE_TYPES` = h1-h6/p/ul/ol/li/blockquote/pre/code/strong/em/a/img/div/table…, `edit-ops.ts:173-174`) and rejects RAG kinds with `edit.create_node: invalid type`. RAG `nodeKind` ('content'|'fact'|'reference', `rag-store.ts:79`) is a SEPARATE field. **CORRECTED 2026-09-08 (verification):** markdown import does NOT mint `nodeKind` either (no `nodeKind` in `markdown-parse.ts`/`markdown-import.ts` — imported nodes are `nodeKind`-undefined ⇒ 'content'); the ONLY place fact/reference nodes are created is the module test suite via direct `store.putNode(makeNode(…, { nodeKind: 'fact' }))` (`tests/unit-x-rag-provenance-traversal.test.ts`). So there is **NO live MCP path that mints fact/reference nodes at all** — the graph-mode provenance probes (S9–S13) and the nodeKind-classified setup (S1–S4) are NOT live-exercisable, not merely "setup not buildable as written". The battery's recorded error string for a bad kind (`rag putNode: nodeKind required/invalid`, battery §3 S2) also does not match the live `edit.create_node: invalid type`. The runnable MCP subset (flat mode, audit log, rag-stream) IS live (live-testing finding F5, 2026-09-08). | Follow the Unit X battery §2 setup (`edit.create_node {type:'fact'}` etc.) against the live app → `edit.create_node: invalid type`; there is no live tool that produces a `nodeKind:'fact'`/`'reference'` node. | `edit.create_node`'s `type` param is the structural `RagNodeType`, not the provenance `RagNodeKind`; the battery conflated the two, and no live surface mints RAG kinds. | Doc/battery fix (NO code change — the design is sound: `create_node` is structural, `nodeKind` is a provenance category): rewrite the Unit X battery to (a) keep the runnable MCP subset (flat mode, audit log, rag-stream) live, and (b) PARK the graph-mode provenance (S9–S13) + the nodeKind-classified setup (S1–S4) as NOT-live-exercisable (no live path mints fact/reference nodes; covered by the module-level greens), the V2-battery A10/A11/B5/B6/C5 pattern. Extending `edit.create_node` to mint RAG kinds is a SEPARATE feature addition, not required to close this finding. |

| **HOST-C1** | high | The coarse line→node map was fabricated (`{ startLine: i, endLine: i + 1 }` — a synthetic 1-line span per RAG object), not real markdown line ranges. | `buildTraversal` on a `ul` with 4 `li` doc-children returned a 1-line range for the `ul`. | The assembly step assigned a synthetic span instead of rendering the envelope to markdown and mapping each subtree's line span. | `src/main/traversal.ts` now renders the envelope to markdown (via `renderProducingProcess` + `MarkdownAdapter`) and maps each RAG subtree's real line span (0-based, inclusive start, exclusive end, COARSE — a `ul`+`li` chunk maps to the whole list; a doc-child's lines map to the doc-child). Regression-tested. |
| **HOST-C2** | medium | A parent's back-reference included its doc-children's node ids (`collectSubtreeIds` walked the whole subtree). | `backRefs.get('ul')` = `[ul, li1, li2, li3, li4]`. | `collectSubtreeIds` descended into every child, including doc-child subtree roots. | `collectSubtreeIds` now stops at each doc-child subtree root (a child carrying the stable `rag-<id>` id) — the parent's owned set EXCLUDES its doc-children's nodes (§5.2 rule 6 / §5.9). Regression-tested. |
| **HOST-C3** | medium | `docChildTargets` was scoped globally, not per document — a cross-document shared node that is a doc-child target in doc A but a multi-parent shared node in doc B was silently dropped in B. | `buildTraversal({ documentIds: ['docB'] })` did not materialize a node that is a doc-child target in doc A. | The doc-child exclusion set was built from ALL `doc-child` edges, not the current document's. | The doc-child exclusion is now scoped to the CURRENT document's doc-child edges (a `doc-child` edge belongs to the document whose node set contains its source). Regression-tested. |
| **HOST-C6** | low | Duplicate `documentIds` entries double-materialized content. | `buildTraversal({ documentIds: ['doc', 'doc'] })` emitted every section twice. | `documentIds` was not deduped. | `documentIds` is deduped (order-preserving) at the top of `buildTraversal`. Regression-tested. |
| **HOST-C7** | low | RAG node `props` (e.g. `href`/`src` for `a`/`img`) were not propagated to the subtree root. | An `a` node with `props: { href: '...' }` rendered without the href. | The subtree root's props were built from only `id` + `data-doc-head`. | The RAG node's own `props` are merged into the subtree root's props (`id` and `data-doc-head` take precedence). Regression-tested. |
| **HOST-UI1** | high | In dark mode (`prefers-color-scheme: dark`), the app shell is unreadable — white text on white backgrounds. | Set the OS/app to dark mode; the header/body/cards show white UA-default text over the hard-coded light `#f4f6f8`/`#fff` backgrounds. | `src/renderer/index.html` sets `:root { color-scheme: light dark }` (UA uses dark-scheme white text) but hard-codes light backgrounds with no dark-mode overrides. | `src/renderer/index.html` now has a `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)` block giving every hard-coded light background/text an equivalent dark value (body `.card` inputs `#status` etc.), honoring the `color-scheme: light dark` declaration. |
| **HOST-UI2** | high | The `SidebarPanes` renderer host NEVER boots in the real Electron app — `Uncaught TypeError: Cannot add property sidebar, object is not extensible` aborts `boot()` before it subscribes to `rag-store-changed`, so no RAG document ever materializes in the app view. | Launch the app with the default `contextIsolation: true`; the renderer console shows the throw and `get_rendered_html` stays on the empty placeholder envelope after an `edit.import_markdown`. | `installSidebarBridge` adds a `sidebar` property to `window.provident`, but in the real renderer `window.provident` is the `contextBridge`-exposed object, which is FROZEN — the property-add throws. The test dom-shim leaves `window.provident` a plain mutable object, so tests never hit it (a test-env vs. real-env divergence). | `src/main/preload.ts` now OWNS the `sidebar` object + exposes `installSidebar(methods)` (each exposed `sidebar.*` method DELEGATES to a holder the renderer installs); `src/renderer/sidebar-panes.ts` `installSidebarBridge` registers via `installSidebar` when present (real app) and falls back to a direct attach only for the non-contextBridge test shim. Boot now completes → subscribes → re-derives → materializes. Regression-tested (a frozen `window.provident` boot test). |
| **HOST-U2-F1** | a-big (high) | `String.fromCodePoint` threw a `RangeError` on out-of-range / lone-surrogate HTML character references, so `decomposeRichHtml` was NOT total (violating its §1.2/§2.2 fail-state 7 contract) — and `sanitizePastedHtml` also threw on the same hostile input. | `decomposeRichHtml('<a href="https://x?a=&#x110000;">link</a>')` (or a `&#xD800;` lone-surrogate ref) threw instead of returning `{ ok: true, ... }`. | `decodeHtmlRefs` (in `src/main/paste-sanitize.ts`, shared by `sanitizePastedHtml` + `decomposeRichHtml`) called `String.fromCodePoint(code)` unguarded for every numeric ref; `fromCodePoint` throws `RangeError` for a code point above `0x10FFFF` or in the surrogate range `0xD800–0xDFFF`. | `decodeHtmlRefs` now guards `code > 0x10ffff || (code >= 0xd800 && code <= 0xdfff)` and leaves the literal ref un-decoded. This makes BOTH `decomposeRichHtml` and `sanitizePastedHtml` total on such inputs (a host defect fix, additive-safe). Regression-tested (Unit U2 adversarial ADR-11 in `tests/unit-u2-rich-decompose.test.ts`). Found in the Unit U2 adversarial pass (RCA-3), recorded as §6 F1. |
| **HOST-U3-F3** | minor | A malformed payload (an empty/absent `content` array) threw a `TypeError` during the post-assembly host passes. | `loadAppGraph` on an envelope whose payload `content` is `[]` (a payload with no root node) threw instead of rendering. | `setTextareaReadOnly` (Unit L §5.3) and the new `applyEditingMode` (Unit U3 §1.3) both dereferenced `p.content[0]` without a guard — a payload with an empty `content` array made `content[0]` `undefined`, and the walk helper's first step threw on it. | Both passes now drive the walk with `walk(p.content?.[0])`, and the `walk` helper starts with `if (!n) return` (the guard lives inside the walk helper, equivalent to guarding the root before recursing — cheap hardening; no behavior change for valid envelopes). This covers BOTH `setTextareaReadOnly` and `applyEditingMode` (a host defect fix). Regression-tested (Unit U3 adversarial F3 in `tests/rich-splice.test.ts` — "a malformed payload does NOT throw in contenteditable mode"). Found in the Unit U3 adversarial pass (RCA-3), recorded as the spec §5 F3. |

|  **FIXED (2026-09-16):** the root cause was in `src/main/traversal.ts` `buildSubtree` — EVERY subtree root (each section AND every nested block) was authored with `placement: { targetPlacement: [zoneName] }`, so `translateLegacy` minted a `content` anchor for the section AND for each nested block; the placement-path enumeration then produced TWO routes for the same node (one through the section, one through the nested child) and the render emitted the child twice — nested inside the section and again as a zone-level sibling. The fix threads a `placed` flag through `buildSubtree`: only a PAYLOAD ROOT (a section, or a genuine multi-parent duplicate) carries the zone `targetPlacement`; a nested subtree is reached through its owner's route and announces none. `renderSubtreeMarkdown` re-adds the anchor on a CLONE for its line-map measurement, so the markdown ranges are unchanged. Tests: `tests/import-render-no-duplicates.test.ts` (NEW 3 tests: per-rag-id element census on a real `/docs/specs` import; the RENDERED doc has no duplicate element ids) — red→green. **LIVE END-TO-END (2026-09-16):** importing `docs/specs/user-flow-audit.md` (`edit.import_markdown`, 110 nodes / 213 edges) and focusing it → the rendered stage has **137 `[data-rag-node-id]` elements with ZERO duplicate rag nodes, ZERO duplicate element ids**, and all 12 ≥50-char source-text probes appear EXACTLY ONCE (0 missing, 0 doubled) — the user's 1-1 requirement holds. Trio 4746 pass / 58 skip / 0 fail.  **FIXED (2026-09-16, `src/renderer/runtime.ts` `tearDownGraph`):** the previous graph's `#wiki-root` ELEMENT outlives its node — `loadEnvelope` replaces the supervisor, so the old root is never destroyed and its element stays in the mount as an in-flow `display:grid` box. `tearDownGraph` now removes every element carrying a graph ROOT id (`#wiki-root`) from the mount BEFORE the new render (the diff-based render can only remove elements it tracks, and a root owned by a discarded supervisor is untracked by def **FIXED (2026-09-16, `tearDownGraph` `#wiki-root` mount sweep — defect F-2)** | **FIXED (2026-09-16, `tearDownGraph` `#wiki-root` mount sweep — defect F-2)** |inition); fail-soft. **LIVE-CONFIRMED:** at boot and after TWO app-graph re-assemblies (a pane-visibility toggle each way) the page holds exactly **ONE `#wiki-root`** with `#app` top at **36px** and `scrollY 0` — previously a childless stale mount (h ≈ 11 589px) sat above the live one and pushed the canvas ~11 000px down.
