| **`SLOT-HOST-ENVELOPE-AUTHORED-CONTAINER-SOURCE`** | FOUNDATION/TREE (not the npm package) | **ANSWERED `2026-10-04` — and THE FILED GAP WAS PARTLY A MISSTATEMENT OF OURS. Verdict `NOT-DELEGABLE` (substantive: no unit admitted), docs-only; their pinning decision row `SLOTHOST-CONTAINER-SOURCE-ADMITS-A-RUNTIME-MATERIALISED-CONTAINER`; their `docs/FORKER.md` §4 recipe `### THE HOST CONTAINER SOURCE — WHAT A FORK SUPPLIES, AND WHEN IT IS CALLED`; gate record `../Provident-Electron/docs/specs/slot-host-envelope-authored-container-source-review.md`. THE HEADLINE: DO NOT RECORD A REFUSAL — `slothost.md` §2.1's container-source clause item 3 ADMITS any value offering a function-valued `appendChild` (DOM element, shim `ShimElement`, plain object; no `instanceof`/tag predicate asserted or admissible), so a runtime-materialised provident-authored container IS ADMITTED BY CONSTRUCTION and the header's “for CALLER-CREATED nodes” describes whose nodes the host places, not what a container may be. THE REAL BLOCKER IS TEMPORAL: `containerFactory` is called only from `syncContainers()`, when the host is driven for that key with a present usable container — never at construction; supply-and-re-supply, or accept the declared `F-6` no-op degradation. OUR ERRORS, CORRECTED: (1) the suite-exclusion reason was misstated — our own lockfile records `dom-shim.js` + dynamic imports of the FOUNDATION's `src/main/security.js`/`src/main/mcp-server.js` (the `runtime.js`/`demo-envelope.js` imports belong to `mount-invariant-guard.test.ts`); (2) “a fork-side factory can only return a caller-created element” is RETRACTED; (3) clause 2's real blocker is the `S-5` row's CONTENT (the foundation's own MCP census: `PINNED_GROUPS` five, `ALL_TOOLS.length === 22`), not “application context”. DECLINED (do not re-ask): a `containerSource`-shaped projection over an authored tree; a caller-supplied key→element map; a suite split. `SLOTHOST-CONTAINER-SOURCE-IS-INJECTED` is NOT amended. **FORK-SIDE DISPOSITION: the ordering obligation is the reason to decline, never the shape; the dossier's reserved rows stay EMPTY; the pin is intact so NO RE-VENDOR IS OWED.** | The slot/owned-list hosts require a **`containerFactory` that returns CALLER-CREATED containers** (`SlotHostOptions.containerFactory`, documented as *"The SOLE container source"*), while this fork's non-shell UI must be **provender-authored envelope data** — so the zone/sidebar containers cannot answer that seam, `containerFactory` is supplied nowhere in this fork, and `createSlotHost` is consumed nowhere. Measured consequence: **12 seam slots over the two hosts = 2 supplied + 1 supplied-but-incompatible + 9 `must be built`, with `containerFactory` the one named obstacle, `U-SLOTHOST`'s moved-half EMPTY**; and the module's own suite is on the fork's **conformance exclusion list** (it imports out-of-set `dom-shim`/`Runtime`/`demo-envelope`), so an adopting fork cannot evaluate it. **THE ASK IS THREE CLAUSES:** (1) a documented container source admissible for envelope-authored consumers, or an explicit refusal; (2) a conformance-includable `slot-host` suite subset, or a declared absence with the reason; (3) a contract clarification of the container's identity/lifecycle and whether `owned-list-host`'s `mount`/`itemFactory` share the limitation. **THE REQUEST DOCUMENT:** `docs/feature-requests/slot-host-envelope-authored-container-source.md`. **FORK-SIDE INTERIM (recorded):** the zone/sidebar replacement keeps its containers fork-authored and files `slot-host` as the **largest explicit NON-ADOPTION with its reason**, never a silent omission; no fork unit patches or duplicates the host. **Revisit condition:** the foundation answers any of the three clauses, or the fork's zone/replacement unit needs the seam concretely (it has dossier room reserved for the identifier rows that form would introduce). |
| **`FOUNDATION-CAPTURE-OPT-IN-UNREACHABLE-THROUGH-THE-COMPOSITION`** | FOUNDATION/TREE (the adopted gesture family — `../Provident-Electron/`; **NOT a fork-side defect and NOT `provident-ssr`-only** — the vendored copies are byte-identical and are NEVER patched, `R-3`/`R-4`) | **OPEN — FILED `2026-10-04` by the `U-ZONE-REPLACEMENT` / `PD-UI-14` gate-4 fix pass. TWO LIMBS, ONE CLASS: the family's own machinery cannot deliver the pointer-capture opt-in it documents.** **(L1 — NO IDENTITY CHANNEL.)** `src/shared/gesture-session.ts` → `EventSource.capturePointer?(element)` is invoked by the session's capture turn as `capturePointer(element)` — the ELEMENT ONLY — while a real DOM's `element.setPointerCapture(pointerId)` REQUIRES the pointer identity, the source is handed no event object on that path, and the identity is not readable off the element. The module's own bytes also carry no DOM capture-method token (its dated `2026-09-27` note says so deliberately), so the ONLY implementation a consumer can write is an argument-less call. **THE MEASURED FAILURE (`RECORDED READING`; measurer: the unit's red set, `tests/unit-zone-replacement-register.test.ts` → `P-SM-zone-repl-4` arm 6):** the fork's call was recorded by the DOM double as `setPointerCapture(undefined)`; a real DOM THROWS for the no-argument call, the throw is swallowed by the family's own declared fail-soft rule (“a throw from it is swallowed … and never fails a gesture”), and the gesture is therefore **NEVER CAPTURED** — so a drag whose pointer leaves the element's box is LOST (the session's tracking listeners are bound on the element alone). **(L2 — THE COMPOSITION DROPS THE OPT-IN.)** `src/shared/gutter.ts` → `createResizeController`'s `attach` passes the session an install-options record built as `{onStart, onMove, onEnd, onCancel}` **with no `capture` member**, and `src/shared/gutter-affordance.ts` declares its own `capturePointer?: boolean` as *“DECLARED AND IGNORED: the capture opt-in cannot be delivered by the composed controller's `attach`, so this module passes no capture field anywhere”*. So a consumer composing EITHER module (`createGutterAffordance` composes a `createResizeController` over the caller's session itself) has **no module-sanctioned route to opt a control into capture** — and the session's per-control install is first-config-wins (`ledgerKnows(element)` refuses a repeat install), so a consumer cannot take the install itself and hand the composed controller the element afterwards either. **FORK-SIDE INTERIM, WHAT ACTUALLY SHIPS (never a module patch):** the fork records the gesture's pointer identity ITSELF (`src/renderer/renderer.ts` → `noteGutterPointerId`, fed by the affordance element's own `pointerdown` listener and by the delegated resolution) and reads it inside its `shellEventSource().capturePointer`, SKIPPING the capture call where no identity was recorded; and it composes the controller over a **seam adapter** (`gutterControlSession`) that merges `capture: true` into the options the controller's `attach` hands the session — i.e. the two SHAPES are adapted fork-side (`§3.4(d)`), no sink is shared and no vendored byte moves. **THE ASK, upstream-owned and NOT designed here:** give the family ONE sanctioned channel for (i) the pointer identity the capture turn needs and (ii) the per-control capture opt-in through the composed controller — e.g. an identity-carrying capture seam (`capturePointer(element, gesture)` / a caller-supplied identity resolver) and a `capture` member on the options `createResizeController.attach` passes to `session.install`. **The foundation's own choice of shape is the ruling; this row files the gap and the two measured consequences.** **LAYER (`RCA-12`): `[SC]`/`[T]` source + red-set reading. NOT app-green, NOT envelope-green.** | Source reads of the three vendored modules (`gesture-session.ts`, `gutter.ts`, `gutter-affordance.ts` — bytes pinned in `vendor/foundation.lock.json`, read-only here) + the red set's driven arm (`P-SM-zone-repl-4` arm 6, whose two offences were the argument-less capture and the lost off-element commit) + the fork-side fixes that carry the gap. **Reproduction:** drive a gutter gesture whose pointer leaves the element's box through the real wiring, on a DOM double that records the capture call and its argument. **(L1)** the capture capability's only argument is the element, while the DOM call it exists to make needs a pointer id the module neither reads nor owns (it reads no field of any event it is handed). **(L2)** the composed controller's `attach` builds the session's per-control options without a `capture` member, and the affordance module records that omission as a declared limitation rather than a bug — so the opt-in is structurally unreachable through the family's own composition. Both are **API-shape gaps in the foundation's own seams**, not consumer errors. **OWNER: THE FOUNDATION / the adjacent tree's own pass** (`AGENTS.md` item 7: catalogue + hand off, NEVER patch; `R-3`/`R-4`: the fifteen vendored modules are byte-identical at the pin and a missing seam is SURFACED, never patched). **Shape:** (i) widen the capture seam so the session can hand the source the identity it already has at establishment (it reads none today, so the shape needs the foundation's own ruling — a caller-supplied resolver is the least invasive); (ii) let the composed controller's `attach` carry the caller's `capture` opt-in into the install options it builds. **UNTIL THEN the fork's interim above ships, and the fork's behaviour is graded (not the module's).** **Full filing:** `docs/HANDOFF.md` → `FOUNDATION-CAPTURE-OPT-IN-UNREACHABLE-THROUGH-THE-COMPOSITION`.
| **`FOUNDATION-INSTALL-COLLISION-ONE-ELEMENT-ONE-SESSION`** | FOUNDATION/TREE (the adopted gesture family — `../Provident-Electron/`; **NOT a fork-side defect and NOT `provident-ssr`-only** — the vendored copies are byte-identical and are NEVER patched, `R-3`/`R-4`) | **OPEN — FILED `2026-10-05` by the `U-ZONE-REPLACEMENT` / `PD-UI-14` filing pass, on the obligation the contract's `§4.0` BLOCKER 3 records as a SECOND, DISTINCT foundation item `OWED` to the filing owner (a different mechanism from the filed capture gap, sharing no citation). ONE CLASS: THE FAMILY'S `install` GUARD ADMITS EXACTLY ONE SESSION PER ELEMENT, AND THE FAMILY'S OWN FACTORIES EACH HOLD THEIR OWN SESSION — so two VENDORED sessions cannot both drive one element.** **THE MECHANISM, READ AT THE VENDORED SOURCE (`VERIFIED-BY-READ`; reader: `U-ZONE-REPLACEMENT`'s contract pass at `§4.0` blocker 1, re-read by this filing pass at the same symbols):** `src/shared/gesture-session.ts` → `installOperation(element, options?)` **opens with `if (ended) return false`, then `if (element === null \|\| element === undefined) return false`, then `if (ledgerKnows(element)) return false`** (the third refusal is the collision), and the module's own comment above its ledger says ***"The ledger is keyed by identity, and only an ATTACHED entry is an installed control."*** **THE GUARD IS TOTAL AND SILENT:** a second `install` of the same element returns the BOOLEAN `false` — no error, no diagnostic, no alternative route. **THE ELEMENT IS THE GESTURE SURFACE, AND THAT IS WHY THIS IS A COLLISION RATHER THAN A RE-INSTALL:** `onDocumentPointerDown` in this repo's `src/renderer/renderer.ts` resolves the gesture element by `closest(GESTURE_SELECTOR)` and installs THAT element on the pane path's session (`session.install(gestureEl, {onMove, onEnd, onCancel})`, guarded by the fork's own `paneInstalled` set) and, on the gutter path, through `gutterControlSession(session)`'s adapter, which forwards the controller's options **with `capture: true`** (`return session.install(element, { ...input, capture: true })`) — so the SAME element (the pane header / the affordance) is the subject of both. (`VERIFIED-BY-READ`; reader: this filing pass, at both landed call sites in `src/renderer/renderer.ts`.) | **THE TWO COMPOSITIONS THE COLLISION BLOCKS, EACH WITH ITS OWN SECOND SESSION:** **(1) `createRelocateSession` OVER THE PANE PATH.** `src/shared/relocate.ts` → `createRelocateSession(options?)` reads its session from the options (`const session = readSession(holder)`) **and keeps its own per-element `ledger: Set<unknown>`** whose `attach(element, hooks?)` refuses with `if (ledger.has(element)) return false` and whose `detach()` refuses while `ledger.size !== 1`. Composing it over the pane path means handing it a session for an element the PANE session already installed — and even where the caller hands it the SAME session object, the element's `install` has already been taken, so `session.install` answers `false` for the relocate controller's own install attempt. **Composing it as a SECOND session over the same element is refused BY THE MODULE'S OWN GUARD, not by policy.** **(2) `createGutterAffordance` OVER THE HOST'S CONTROLLER.** `src/shared/gutter-affordance.ts` → `createGutterAffordance(options?)` reads **its own session** (`const session = read('session')`) and its own element (`const element = read('element')`) from the options, i.e. it too is an element-installing composer; the contract's `§4.0` BLOCKER 2 records the three further reasons that composition does not fit this unit (`attach` would displace the controller the register's row-4 arms drive; it would need a NEW public write seam because `commitGutterSize` is private; and `applyPreview` has no fork supplier) — and this row is the FOURTH, STRUCTURAL one that sits UNDER those three: **the element can hold exactly one install, so the composition would REPLACE the host's controller's install rather than sit beside it.** **THE CONSEQUENCE, STATED AS A BLOCKER AND NOT AS A PREFERENCE:** composing either session is not an addition to the pane/gutter path — it is a **REWRITE of that path's establishment**, which is why `U-ZONE-REPLACEMENT` records both as `DECLINED FOR THIS UNIT — RECORDED` (`§3.5`/`§3.6`) and routes the relocate half to `PD-UI-4a`'s declared REWRITE (`§4` item (vi)). **THE FOUNDATION'S OWN TEXT DOES NOT RECORD THIS AS AN OBLIGATION:** the `install` guard is documented as a control-ledger rule (one control, one installed handler) and **no clause of the adopted set tells a consumer that the rule is PER ELEMENT ACROSS SESSIONS, or what a consumer with two mechanisms over one surface is supposed to do.** | **THE LEDGER IS KEYED BY ELEMENT IDENTITY AND IS PER SESSION INSTANCE:** `ledgerKnows` is a linear scan of THAT session's `entries` array (`if (entry.element === element) return true`), so the refusal is scoped to one `createGestureSession` instance — and every vendored composer (`createRelocateSession`, `createGutterAffordance`) that must drive an element reads/keeps a session of its own rather than accepting the one that already installed it. **So the family's composition story assumes ONE session per element, while its composition FACTORIES are each built to own one** — and the two assumptions meet only where a consumer wires a single mechanism to a surface. **WHAT IS *NOT* THE PROBLEM, stated so the ask cannot be misfiled as a bug report:** the guard is **CORRECT for its stated job** (a double install would double-bind the `pointerdown` handler and the family's fail-soft rule would hide the second binding's effects), **no module is wrong**, and **this repo must not patch it** (`AGENTS.md` item 7; `R-3`/`R-4`). **WHAT IS MISSING IS A DECLARED ROUTE FOR THE LEGAL SECOND MECHANISM:** either a way to SHARE one element's install across two mechanisms, or a documented statement that a second mechanism over one element must reuse the first mechanism's session — neither of which the adopted set publishes. | **OWNER: THE FOUNDATION (`../Provident-Electron/`)** — **filed upstream as a HANDOFF; the package/foundation and the vendored modules are NEVER patched from this repo (`AGENTS.md` item 7; `R-3`/`R-4`).** **PROPOSED FIX SHAPE, UPSTREAM-OWNED, THREE ADMISSIBLE OUTCOMES, EACH PURELY ADDITIVE:** **(A) A DECLARED REUSE ROUTE — the smallest change:** let a composer accept an ALREADY-INSTALLED element's session (a `session` seam, or an `install` that answers `true` when the SAME element is already attached to the SAME session with a compatible options record), so a second mechanism can ride the first install instead of being refused by it; the guard's own job (one binding per element) is preserved by construction, because the element's binding is not re-taken. **(B) A MULTI-MECHANISM INSTALL — a composable options record:** give `installOperation`'s ledger an explicit fan-out (a declared list of per-mechanism handlers, or a documented `attach` that ADDS a second handler to an existing attached entry), so `ledgerKnows` can distinguish *"already installed by THIS mechanism"* (still a refusal) from *"already installed by ANOTHER mechanism"* (admissible). **(C) AN EXPLICIT UPSTREAM RULING THAT ONE SESSION PER ELEMENT IS BY DESIGN**, stating the consumer-side recipe in the fork-facing carry (`docs/FORKER.md`): *"a second mechanism over one element MUST reuse that element's existing session; a composer that owns its own session may therefore never share an element with another composer"* — which would **CLOSE THIS ROW AS *"DECLARED OUT OF SCOPE"*** with the pane path's `paneInstalled` bookkeeping named as the sanctioned consumer pattern. **Either outcome is acceptable to the consumer; what is NOT acceptable is silence, because the refusal is a silent BOOLEAN and a consumer cannot tell a deliberate one-session-per-element rule from an un-shipped composition route.** **UNBLOCKED CONSEQUENCE FOR US (recorded so the next unit's first act is not re-derived):** until one of (A)/(B)/(C) lands, `PD-UI-4a`'s relocate rewrite and the affordance composition stay `DECLINED`, `U-ZONE-REPLACEMENT`'s `§4.0` blockers 1/2 stand as filed, and the fork's interim (its OWN single session per control element, `§5 G-6`'s per-control wiring) ships and is graded instead. **Full filing:** `docs/HANDOFF.md` → `FOUNDATION-INSTALL-COLLISION-ONE-ELEMENT-ONE-SESSION`.
